  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Genghis Khan, leader of Mongolia (Player 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Mongolia
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Khan, Replacing Great General (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Unit: Black Tug, Replacing Knight (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Unique Building: Ger, Replacing Smokehouse (Requires technology: Trapping)
        - Civilization Ability: Skirmisher Units have an extra Attack. Gain All Yields equal to 20% of the Gold from bullying City-States.
      - Leader: Genghis Khan
      - PlayerID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      - Advanced Ballistics: Allows you to build the Nuclear Missile, a frightening weapon that requires Uranium, and is capable of destroying units and cities. Also allows you to build the Guided Missile, an inexpensive single-use Unit that can damage enemy Units, and the SS Booster, one of the Spaceship parts necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Lasers, Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Elite Forces (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Elite Forces: Newly created Military Units receive +15 Experience. Military Units gain 50% more Experience from combat.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Martial Law (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * No Gold Maintenance from Garrisons.
        - * +1 Happiness and +4 Culture for each Garrison.
        - * -20% Yield penalties of Puppeted Cities.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Justice (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - * Cities with a garrison gain +25% Ranged Combat Strength.
        - * Royal Guardhouse built in Capital (+3 Production, +2 Defense, +50 Hit Points, +10% Military Supply from Population in all Cities, 1 Engineer Slot).
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Foreign Service (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 100 Spy Points.
        - * Earn Great Diplomats 50% faster.
        - * +50% Rewards from City-State Quests.
        - * +1 of every Strategic Resource for every three City-State Alliances you maintain.

    - #0:
      - role: user
      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 9
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 4
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 8
        - Boldness: 8
        - WarBias: 9
        - HostileBias: 8
        - WarmongerHate: 2
        - NeutralBias: 6
        - FriendlyBias: 6
        - GuardedBias: 5
        - AfraidBias: 4
        - DiplomaticBalance: 3
        - Friendliness: 6
        - WorkWithWillingness: 10
        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 10
        - Loyalty: 10
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 6
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 5
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 4
        - MinorCivWarBias: 4
        - DenounceWillingness: 3
        - Forgiveness: 5
        - Meanness: 6
        - Neediness: 5
        - Chattiness: 5
        - DeceptiveBias: 6
        - Rationale: Increasing Boldness (7→8), WarBias (8→9), HostileBias (7→8), and VictoryCompetitiveness (8→9) after Longcheng and Portugal inflicted combat losses. Longcheng killed my Anti-Aircraft Gun and Ironclad with fortified defenses; Portugal's naval forces sank my Ironclad and Austria's destroyer. Portugal's Apollo-complete Science victory pursuit requires more aggressive response. Must take calculated risks to break stalemates and prevent Portugal's victory path. Coalition with Germany/Austria/Celts holding 55 votes (vs Portugal 32) can support bolder tactics through coordinated pressure in 6-turn World Congress timeframe.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Advanced Ballistics
        - Rationale: Advanced Ballistics will unlock Nuclear Missiles (requires Uranium - 8 available) and Guided Missiles for strategic deterrence against Portugal's overwhelming victory threats. Portugal has 35 Congress votes, 7/7 cultural influence, and 16% spaceship progress. Nuclear capability provides the only reliable counter to Portugal's multi-victory path. Once unlocked, Portugal's cities (especially Lisbon with UN) become priority nuclear targets to eliminate their diplomatic, cultural, and science victory potential. This technology is essential for conquest strategy against a scientifically superior opponent.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: None (Policy)
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Barbarians
        - Public: -20
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: Increasing hostility toward Barbarians (default 0 to -20/-30) after they captured our settler last turn. This signals to AI that barbarian elimination is a priority, especially near our territory and protected city-states. The settler loss was costly, and active barbarian threats near Yerevan require aggressive response. This ensures military resources focus on neutralizing barbarian incursions while maintaining primary conquest objectives.
        - UpdatedTurn: 170
        
        ### Yerevan
        - Public: -20
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: Yerevan killed our Tercio in turn 293 and is currently at War (-60 influence). Portugal has a Colonist near Yerevan threatening to ally them, which would give Portugal access to our central territory. Increasing hostility to signal AI that Yerevan must be dealt with before Portugal secures them. We need to either capture Yerevan or ensure Germany's influence remains dominant to prevent Portuguese expansion.
        - UpdatedTurn: 295
        
        ### Msoura
        - Public: -30
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: Adjusting Msoura relationship to -30/-30 (from default 0/0) to reflect reality. Venice's Great Diplomat expended in Msoura (turn 339) caused our influence to drop from 36 to -60, triggering 'War - Peace Currently Blocked' status. Hostile stance acknowledges this permanent relationship shift under current game state. This signals to AI that Msoura is no longer friendly and diplomatic actions toward them require aggression or must be abandoned entirely. Venice's action of spending a Great Diplomat to sabotage my 36 influence demonstrates their priority in controlling city-states near the Msoura region.
        - UpdatedTurn: 341
        
        ### Venice
        - Public: -5
        - Private: -5
        - Rationale: Adjusting Venice relationship slightly (-10/-15 to -5/-5). Events show unusual war declaration on my vassal Venice, but this may be a display issue or temporary. Reducing hostility acknowledges they remain formally my capitulated vassal despite confusing war declarations. Venice provides valuable naval support and continues fighting common enemies (Polynesia, Portugal). Keep relationship moderately cautious while not punishing our vassal for potential game error.
        - UpdatedTurn: 389
        
        ### Babylon
        - Public: 15
        - Private: 15
        - Rationale: Reducing Babylon relationship from 25/20 to 15/15. Babylon is Portugal's voluntary vassal providing 5 Congress votes to Portugal's 32-vote dominance. While I explored diplomatic opportunities, Babylon's continued support of my primary enemy warrants signal of disfavor. Portugal's aggression (sinking my Tank, Longcheng attacks) demonstrates Babylon's alignment against coalition interests. This adjustment maintains some flexibility but acknowledges their hostile stance.
        - UpdatedTurn: 390
        
        ### Polynesia
        - Public: -10
        - Private: -10
        - Rationale: Increasing Polynesia hostility from -5/-5 to -10/-10 after recent naval combat losses. Portugal's Battleship sank my Tank near Sigtuna while attacking Polynesian territory, and ongoing naval engagements show Polynesia actively opposing coalition despite initial -5 setting. This maintains focus on Portugal as primary threat but acknowledges Polynesia's hostile military posture. Not increasing further to avoid overcommitment against secondary enemy.
        - UpdatedTurn: 390
        
        ### Portugal
        - Public: -40
        - Private: -45
        - Rationale: Increasing Portugal hostility from -35/-40 to -40/-45 after Portugal's nuclear positioning near central theater, continued naval superiority (Fleet Destroyers in multiple zones), and Portugal's Apollo-complete Science victory pursuit. XCOM Squad deployment in their territory unit points to advanced aggression direction. Portugal has 32 Congress votes and dominates city-states - signals to AI maximum hostility required while maintaining coalition blocking capability. Maximum hostility reflects Portugal as primary victory threat requiring decisive response through military pressure and nuclear deterrence.
        - UpdatedTurn: 392
        
        ### Germany
        - Public: 65
        - Private: 65
        - Rationale: Increasing Germany relationship (60/60 to 65/65) after Longcheng capture success and their Great Diplomat/Inquisitor movement in the area. World Congress vote critical next turn - Germany's Autocracy proposal and Sphere of Influence (Mogadishu) must pass. Germany's 62,024 military strength overwhelming but bers - maintain maximum alliance. Germany provided Infantry support during Longcheng siege; increased relationship rewards cooperation and ensures voting alignment against Portugal.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### Austria
        - Public: 50
        - Private: 50
        - Rationale: Increasing Austria relationship (45/46 to 50/50) after their Sanction Babylon proposal (anti-Portugal alliance) and continued military support. Austria's 5,243 military provides crucial coalition presence. Longcheng capture demonstrates effectiveness; Austria's 19 Congress votes essential for blocking Portugal's Kuala Lumpur Sphere of Influence next turn. Austria's Inquisitor earlier showed activity near theater - increased relationship ensures continued anti-Portugal voting.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### The Celts
        - Public: 25
        - Private: 25
        - Rationale: Increasing Celts relationship (18/18 to 25/25) after their Destroyers engaged enemy forces in Hilo (Event 48) and ongoing war front against Portugal. Celts' 7,245 military provides pressure on Portugal's eastern flank, forcing Portugal to split forces. Longcheng capture shows coalition working; Celts' 8 votes needed next turn for World Congress. Slight increase rewards coordination against mutual enemy Portugal.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Maintaining current strategies during critical pre-nuclear phase. Portugal's Global Hegemony proposals threaten diplomatic victory in 2 turns. Coalition (Germany 20 + Austria 19 + Celts 13 + Mongolia 11 = 63 vs Portugal 35) votes effectively without changes needed. Persona settings (VictoryCompetitiveness 9, WarBias 9, Boldness 8) remain appropriate for aggressive posture against Portugal. Diplomatic relationships (Germany +65, Austria +50, Celts +25) strong for coordination. Nuclear Fission research (7 turns) and Advanced Ballistics path unchanged. Lost Ironclad to Polynesia but Longcheng victory honors ancestor spirits - naval losses acceptable while advancing nuclear capabilities. Golden Age in 3 turns will offset unhappy combat penalty for upcoming offensives.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 42
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 1 turn
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Portuguese Sphere of Influence: Sigtuna:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Autocracy:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Portuguese Sphere of Influence: Quebec City:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Science Initiative:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians.
              Civilizations with Scores from Technologies and Future Tech below the global median will receive a boost to Food and Science up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Salem:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Sofia:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: Babylon:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Global Hegemony:
            - Description: Designate a player that could achieve Global Hegemony. If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory. The number of Delegates needed depends on the number of Civilizations and City-States in the game, and can be seen on the World Congress screen.
              If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates to use in future sessions.
              May not be proposed by a Civilization. Automatically proposed at regular intervals once a World Ideology Resolution is active.
            - Proposer: Unknown

        You, Genghis Khan (leader of Mongolia, Player 4), are making strategic decisions after turn 407.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Genghis Khan, leader of Mongolia (Opponent 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Mongolia
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Khan, Replacing Great General (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Unit: Black Tug, Replacing Knight (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Unique Building: Ger, Replacing Smokehouse (Requires technology: Trapping)
        - Civilization Ability: Skirmisher Units have an extra Attack. Gain All Yields equal to 20% of the Gold from bullying City-States.
      - Leader: Genghis Khan
      - OpponentID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      - Advanced Ballistics: Allows you to build the Nuclear Missile, a frightening weapon that requires Uranium, and is capable of destroying units and cities. Also allows you to build the Guided Missile, an inexpensive single-use Unit that can damage enemy Units, and the SS Booster, one of the Spaceship parts necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Lasers, Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Elite Forces (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Elite Forces: Newly created Military Units receive +15 Experience. Military Units gain 50% more Experience from combat.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Martial Law (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * No Gold Maintenance from Garrisons.
        - * +1 Happiness and +4 Culture for each Garrison.
        - * -20% Yield penalties of Puppeted Cities.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Justice (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - * Cities with a garrison gain +25% Ranged Combat Strength.
        - * Royal Guardhouse built in Capital (+3 Production, +2 Defense, +50 Hit Points, +10% Military Supply from Population in all Cities, 1 Engineer Slot).
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Foreign Service (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 100 Spy Points.
        - * Earn Great Diplomats 50% faster.
        - * +50% Rewards from City-State Quests.
        - * +1 of every Strategic Resource for every three City-State Alliances you maintain.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - TargetID: 3
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: Maximum hostility toward Portugal is essential. They have completed the SS Engine, bringing them to three spaceship parts and advancing their Science Victory. Their 35 delegates and imminent diplomatic gains from Global Hegemony make them the existential threat. This signals to staff to reject any peace overtures and prioritize Portugal's destruction.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - TargetID: 7
        - Public: 70
        - Private: 70
        - Rationale: Boosting alliance with Germany to 70/70 reinforces our coalition anchor. Germany's overwhelming military and 18 delegates are vital for World Congress coordination and the eventual joint offensive against Portugal. Maintaining this bond ensures continued joint operations and voting alignment.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - TargetID: 1
        - Public: 55
        - Private: 55
        - Rationale: Increasing Austria to 55/55 rewards their sustained anti-Portugal front and Sanction Babylon proposal. Their 19 delegates and territorial pressure on Portugal's northern flank remain indispensable. Stronger ties encourage continued military cooperation and block Portugal's expansion.
    - #3:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - TargetID: 6
        - Public: 30
        - Private: 30
        - Rationale: Raising Celts to 30/30 acknowledges their naval engagement against Portugal and Polynesia. Their 12 delegates add crucial weight to coalition voting. Solidifying this friendship keeps pressure on Portugal's eastern front and secures another anti-Portugal voice in the Congress.
    - #4:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - TargetID: 5
        - Public: 10
        - Private: 10
        - Rationale: Adjusting Venice to a modest positive 10/10 reflects their continued service as our capitulated vassal and their active combat against Portugal and Polynesia. Encouraging loyalty ensures their 13 delegates and trade network continue to serve Mongol interests.
    - #5:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Policy: Honor
        - Rationale: Honor is the highest-priority policy available. It retroactively grants supply-free military units near Karakorum, Beshbalik, and Otrar, instantly expanding our field army. The +10% Combat Strength to all units and -25% War Weariness directly improve our stalled offensives and overall combat posture while we complete the nuclear tech path.
    - #6:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
        - Flavors:
          - CityDefense: 70
          - UseNuke: 95
          - Happiness: 95
          - Gold: 90
      - rationale: The empire is on the brink of nuclear readiness and must survive Portugal's multi-front superiority until then. CityDefense is raised to 70 to garrison against XCOM and Rocket Artillery raids. UseNuke increased to 95 to guarantee immediate launch of our first Nuclear Missiles the moment Advanced Ballistics completes. Happiness pushed to 95 to cure our current unhappiness combat penalty. Gold increased to 90 to fund upgrades and missile production without stalling.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  We need to respond with tool calls as Genghis Khan after turn 407. Let's analyze the situation carefully.
            
            First, existing strategies:
            - GrandStrategy: Conquest
            - Flavors: Offense 90, Defense 45, Mobilization 95, CityDefense 50, MilitaryTraining 90, Recon 50, Ranged 85, Mobile 80, Nuke 100, UseNuke 80, Naval 65, Antiair 90, Production 95, Science 100, Happiness 85, Diplomacy 95, Espionage 85, etc.
            - Technology: Next Advanced Ballistics (currently researching Nuclear Fission, estimated 6 turns)
            - Policy: None currently set.
            - Relationships: Germany +65/+65, Austria +50/+50, Celts +25/+25, Portugal -40/-45, Polynesia -10/-10, Babylon +15/+15, Venice -5/-5, Yerevan -20/-30, Msoura -30/-30.
            
            Victory Progress:
            - Domination: Need 8 capitals. Have Karakorum, Venice (2). Portugal has Babylon, Lisbon (2).
            - Science: Portugal is contender with Apollo complete, 33% parts (2 completed), 7/7 influences culturally. They are building SS Engine in Lisbon (just completed project). They are dangerously close to Science victory.
            - Cultural: Portugal influential over everyone (7/7). They have 100% policies. They could potentially build Citizen Earth Protocol wonder? Need two Tier 3 tenets and finish that wonder. But Portugal is Order ideology, has 5 points in Order. They may not have two Tier 3 tenets yet? Actually they have Tradition 2, Rationalism 6, Statecraft 6, Order 5, Progress 6, Industry 3. Order 5 means they have 5 tenets in Order (but only up to level 2? Let's see: level 1 tenets require 2 policies in branch, level 2 requires 4, level 3 requires 6. So with 5 they have up to two level 2 tenets and one level 1? Actually in VP ideology, points are tenets? Wait, "PolicyBranches: Order: 5" means they have adopted 5 policies in Order. That could be 2 level-1, 2 level-2, 1 level-3? Actually each tenet costs 1 policy point but tier restrictions apply: you need 2 policies to unlock Tier 2, 4 policies to unlock Tier 3. With 5 policies, they could have 1 Tier 3, 2 Tier 2, 2 Tier 1? Or something. The point is they might be close to cultural victory but need to build Citizen Earth Protocol. They have tons of tourism. But they also have Apollo.
            - Diplomatic: Votes needed 42. Portugal has 35 delegates, 83% victory percentage. Next turn is World Congress voting with Global Hegemony on the table. Coalition (Germany 18 + Austria 19 + Celts 12 + Mongolia 11 = 60) could outvote Portugal's 35 (Babylon has 5, Venice has 13? Actually Venice is my vassal but they have 13 delegates? Wait Venice has 13 delegates. They are my capitulated vassal - do they vote for me? Or for themselves? In VP, vassals typically vote for their master? Actually the diplomatic victory description says vassals cannot achieve domination victory before independence, but for UN votes, vassals might vote for their master? The game UI might show Venice as having 13 delegates. But are those available to me? Probably not directly, but they might vote with me. However, the current active proposals include Global Hegemony (automatically proposed). Portugal has 35, us 11, Germany 18, Austria 19, Celts 12, Venice 13, Polynesia 7, Babylon 5. Total delegates = 120? Let's sum: 35+11+18+19+12+13+7+5 = 120. Votes needed 42. So Portugal needs 42. Without some allies, they can't get 42. They might get Babylon (5) = 40. Maybe Venice? But Venice is my vassal and at war with Portugal? Actually Venice is at war with Portugal (score 0% war weariness). So Venice likely won't vote for Portugal. If coalition votes against Portugal, Portugal cannot win. However, the top two civs who receive most support permanently gain 5 delegates. That could be dangerous because Portugal could gain +5 delegates if they are top or second. We need to make sure either we or an ally gets more support than Portugal, or we block it. With coalition 60 vs 35, we can vote "No" or support someone else. The best move might be to vote for Germany or Austria to prevent Portugal from being top. Since our relationship with Germany and Austria is maxed, we should coordinate. But we don't have direct control over voting; staff members decide based on our relationships and persona. Since we have Diplomacy flavor 95 and high alliance values, the AI will likely vote with allies. But we need to ensure we don't accidentally give Portugal delegates. Actually the proposal is Global Hegemony: "Designate a civilization that could achieve Global Hegemony. If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory." We can vote for someone else or abstain? In Civ5, you can vote for a candidate or against all? In VP, I think you vote for a specific civ. Portugal will likely vote for themselves. The coalition should vote for someone else (e.g., Germany or Austria) to ensure Portugal doesn't get top votes. But our staff members might decide based on flavors. Since our Diplomacy flavor is 95, they will try to use diplomats etc. But we don't have direct control over voting.
            
            The key threats:
            1. Portugal has Apollo, 2/6 parts (33%), and just completed SS Engine (project). That means they have now 3/6? Wait events say: "CityProjectComplete: SS Engine". That adds one part. The Victory Progress says PartsCompleted: 2, PartsPercentage: 33%. That was before this turn? Actually the report may be after turn 407 events. Let's check: Victory Progress says Portugal: PartsPercentage: 33, PartsCompleted: 2, ApolloComplete: 1. Turn 407 event: "CityProjectComplete: SS Engine". That would make it 3 parts, 50%. But the report might not have updated. Let's be safe: assume they now have 3 parts. They could win in a few more parts. They need 6 total. So we have limited time.
            2. Portugal is building spaceship parts and has huge tech lead (82 techs vs our 64). We cannot out-science them. Our only hope is to stop them militarily.
            3. We are researching Nuclear Fission (6 turns left). Then we need Advanced Ballistics (which was set as next). That unlocks Nuclear Missiles and Guided Missiles. We have 8 Uranium. With Nuclear Missiles, we can nuke Portuguese cities to halt production and perhaps capture them? But Lisbon has 45 pop, Defense 80, but wonder-filled. Braga has 120 defense. Their cities are strong.
            4. We have military strength 6296, Portugal 10061. But we have a coalition: Germany 67360 (!), Austria 3666, Celts 7199. Germany's military is enormous. However, Germany's war with Portugal shows score 0% war weariness, meaning they are stalemated or not really fighting. We need to leverage coalition strength.
            5. We are unhappy (combat penalty). Golden Age in 2 turns. That will help.
            6. Our vassal Venice is active, producing units, plundering Polynesian trade routes.
            7. We have many spies. One is traveling to Salzburg (Portugal). Others in Polynesia, Babylon, Lisbon. They are gathering intelligence. We might want to switch spy roles to rig elections in city-states or steal tech, but city-state influence is mostly blocked (war - peace blocked) for many. Actually many city-states are Portugal's allies but we are at war with them. So we cannot rig elections while at war? I'm not sure. Spies can do counterintelligence or steal tech. We are behind in tech, so stealing from Portugal (Lisbon) would be huge. Our spy Odtsetseg (network 290) is in Lisbon. That's low network. Maybe should move to a different city? Or we could use spies to stage coups in city-states? But we are at war with many city-states (War - Peace Currently Blocked). Actually the city-state relationships show "War - Peace Currently Blocked" for many, meaning we cannot make peace until a certain time. So those city-states are permanently hostile? Actually "War - Peace Currently Blocked" could mean peace is blocked for some turns. But it's been like that for many turns.
            
            Let's think about strategic priorities:
            
            - **Science/Tech**: We must get Nuclear Missiles ASAP. Advanced Ballistics is next after Nuclear Fission (7 turns). We already set that. We should ensure we don't get distracted. Should we change next tech? The options include Refrigeration, Penicillin, Advanced Ballistics. Advanced Ballistics is already chosen. Good.
            - **Policy**: We have no next policy set. We have 21 turns until next policy (but with culture output 1226, maybe faster if we take cities or get great works). Actually NextPolicyTurns: 21. So not immediate. But when it comes, we should have a plan. Our ideology is Autocracy, with Military-Industrial Complex, United Front, Police State. We have 3 tenets in Autocracy. Could get a Tier 2 or Tier 3 soon. Options include Elite Forces, Lebensraum, Futurism, New World Order, Iron Fist. All are Level 1 Tenets of Autocracy? Wait the options list:
              - Elite Forces (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy)
              - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy)
              - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy)
              - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy)
              - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy)
            But then there are continuing branches: Honor (Authority), Regimental System (Imperialism), Organization (Progress), Martial Law (Imperialism), Civilizing Mission (Imperialism), Fraternity (Progress), Trade Confederacy (Statecraft), Ceremony (Tradition), Nobility (Fealty), Justice (Tradition), Scientific Revolution (Rationalism), Free Trade (Industry), Division of Labor (Industry), Refinement (Artistry), Enlightenment (Rationalism), Foreign Service (Statecraft).
            
            We currently have: Tradition (Sovereignty), Rationalism (Rationalism), Progress (Liberty, Equality), Authority (Tribute, Imperium, Militarism, Dominance), Fealty (Fealty), Industry (Industry), Artistry (Humanism), Statecraft (Statecraft), Autocracy (Military-Industrial Complex, United Front, Police State), Imperialism (Imperialism, Colonialism).
            
            Our next policy is in 21 turns. We should choose something that helps war effort or blocks Portugal. Options from Autocracy Level 1: but we might already have 3 tenets (Military-Industrial Complex, United Front, Police State). Are those Level 1? Let's see: In Autocracy tree, Level 1 tenets are typically: Military-Industrial Complex, United Front, Police State, Elite Forces, Lebensraum, Futurism, New World Order, Iron Fist, Mobilization, etc. Actually in VP Autocracy has many tenets. But the options listed are all Level 1. Wait, maybe the game treats them as Level 1 for the purpose of this list because we haven't unlocked higher tiers? No, usually in ideology, tenets have tiers. But the policy list might be showing all available tenets we can take next. In VP, after taking 3 tenets in an ideology, you can take higher tier ones. But the list only shows Level 1? That seems odd. Let's re-read: The # Options section lists policies available:
            - Elite Forces (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy)
            - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy)
            - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy)
            - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy)
            - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy)
            - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch)
            ...
            So maybe the game is offering Level 1 Autocracy tenets because we haven't adopted them yet. Wait, but we already adopted Military-Industrial Complex, United Front, Police State. Those are also Level 1? If so, we have 3 Level 1 tenets, but there are more Level 1 tenets available. So we can take more Level 1 tenets before needing higher tiers? Actually no, in Civ5 you can take any tenet in any order as long as you have enough points and meet tier requirements? In VP, you can take 2 level 1, then unlock level 2 after 2, level 3 after 4? Something like that. If we have 3 tenets, we might have unlocked level 2, but the list doesn't show level 2. So perhaps the game only lists what's currently available. Actually the options might be limited by what's actually possible to pick right now. But the text says "Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy" for all 5. Could it be that the game hasn't updated the tier labels? Or that we can only pick Level 1 because we need to pick all? Let's assume we can pick any of these.
            
            Which policy should we pick for next? We need something that helps military might or prevents Portugal's victory.
            - Elite Forces: +15 XP for new units, +50% XP from combat. Good for war but we already have many units.
            - Lebensraum: 10 Culture and GAP when borders expand. Citadels in foreign territory. Retain tiles after razing. Not urgent.
            - Futurism: +2 Culture from Great Works, +50 Tourism when conquer city. Helps tourism but not critical.
            - New World Order: -2 Unhappiness/Distress. Police Stations and Constabularies +3 Culture/+5 Production, built 100% faster. Good for happiness and production. We are unhappy (48%). This could help. Also gives culture.
            - Iron Fist: Vassals cannot be liberated, capitulated vassals cannot rebel. +25% yields from city-states, vassals tribute extra, +50% worker improvement rate. Venice is our capitulated vassal; this prevents rebellion and boosts yields. Also prevents Portugal from liberating Venice. Very strong! Especially since Venice is a vassal and provides trade routes and units. Prevents liberation by other civs (Portugal coalition). This is extremely valuable diplomatically and economically. Iron Fist seems like best choice.
            
            Other continuing branches:
            - Honor: All military units +10% CS. Supply-free units at multiples of 10 pop. -25% war weariness. Good but we are Authority already maxed? Actually Authority branch is fully filled (5 policies). Honor is a continuing branch after Authority? Wait Honor is listed as "Continuing Authority Branch". In VP, after finishing a branch, you get continuing policies. We have Authority (5 policies: Authority, Tribute, Imperium, Militarism, Dominance). So Honor is available as a continuing policy. +10% CS to all military units is huge. And supply-free units. We are at war with many. War weariness reduction helps.
            - Martial Law (Continuing Imperialism): No gold maintenance from garrisons, +1 Happiness +4 Culture per garrison, -20% yield penalties of puppeted cities. We have Imperialism (Imperialism, Colonialism). Not finished? Imperialism has 2 policies in the report. Actually "Imperialism: 2" means we have 2 policies in Imperialism. Wait the report says under PolicyBranches: "Imperialism: Imperialism, Colonialism". That's 2. So we haven't finished Imperialism branch. Therefore Martial Law and others are not available until we finish the branch. The list includes them but maybe we can't actually pick them. The options should reflect what we can pick. But let's be cautious. The list says "Continuing Imperialism Branch" but we have only 2 policies there. Usually you need to finish the branch to unlock continuing policies. But maybe in VP you don't need to finish? Actually the game says "Continuing Authority Branch" etc. It might mean they are available because we opened the branch? That seems odd. Let's check: we have Authority fully filled (5). So Honor should be available. We have Imperialism not filled (2), so Martial Law, Regimental System, Civilizing Mission might not be available. But they are listed as options. Since the tool requires us to pick from options, we can pick any listed. However, we should pick something from ideology or a branch we have invested in.
            
            Given the critical situation: Iron Fist is extremely powerful. It secures Venice permanently, increases city-state and vassal yields. Since Portugal might try to liberate Venice or incite rebellion, securing our vassal is top priority. Also the +25% yields from city-states and extra vassal tribute helps our economy. I think Iron Fist is the best next policy.
            
            Alternatively, Scientific Revolution (Rationalism) gives +6 Science observatories and +2 from jungle/snow. But we need military more.
            
            Foreign Service (Statecraft) gives 100 spy points, +50% rewards from city-state quests, +1 strategic resource per 3 city-state alliances. We have some city-state friendships but many are at war.
            
            New World Order helps happiness and production. But our happiness is 48% (unhappy). NWO would give -2 unhappiness from distress in all cities and speed up police stations/constabularies. We have many cities with constabularies already. It could help but Iron Fist seems more strategic.
            
            Honor gives +10% combat strength to all units. That's massive. Combined with our already strong military, this could help us push. But Iron Fist is ideology and has broader strategic value for vassal security and economy. Which is better? We are heavily reliant on Venice. If Venice rebels, we lose 13 votes and a major ally. Preventing liberation is key. Also we have many puppeted/occupied cities. Iron Fist's +50% worker improvement rate helps rebuild. I think Iron Fist is slightly better. But let's consider: we have 21 turns until next policy, so this won't affect immediately. By then, the world might have changed. Still, setting it now ensures we don't waste a turn.
            
            Actually, looking at the events: Venice is building units, improving, and is our vassal. They seem loyal. But in VP, capitulated vassals can rebel if they have high enough liberty desire or if another civ liberates them. Portugal is at war with Venice. If Portugal captures Venice's original capital (Venice city), they might liberate it? Wait Venice's capital is Venice. If Portugal captures it, they could choose to keep or liberate? In Civ5, you can't liberate a capital of a civ that still exists (Venice exists). Actually Venice is a major civ but is a vassal. If an enemy captures Venice city, they could return it to Venice (freeing them?) or keep it. Iron Fist says "Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel." So this completely removes that risk. Very strong.
            
            Let me check the exact wording of our current policy list: Iron Fist: "Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate." Yes, that's huge.
            
            So I will call set-policy for Iron Fist.
            
            - **Relationships**: Do we need to adjust relationships?
              - Portugal: Currently -40/-45. Next turn is World Congress vote. Should we increase hostility further to ensure AI votes against them? Maybe -45/-50 to signal absolute enmity. But our staff already likely votes against them.
              - Germany: 65/65. Should we increase? Max is 100. Germany is our top ally. Maybe keep as is.
              - Austria: 50/50. Keep.
              - Celts: 25/25. Could increase to ensure they vote with coalition. The Celts are at war with Portugal and have 12 votes. Let's bump to 30/30 to solidify.
              - Venice: -5/-5. They are our vassal but at war with Polynesia and Portugal. We might want to increase positive relationship with Venice? Actually our opinion to them is Friendly already due to ideology match and vassalage. Our public/private opinion is -5/-5. Since they are our vassal, negative public opinion might be confusing staff. Should we set to positive? But they are "completely untrustworthy" to us due to warmongering? Wait TheirOpinionToUs says "We are completely untrustworthy to them." Our opinion to them is Friendly. The public/private opinion we set adds to this. Currently -5. We could set to +10/+10 to encourage treating Venice better. However, the game might treat vassal differently. Still, positive modifiers could help if we want to ensure Venice stays loyal and votes with us. But Venice is capitulated vassal, they may not have choice. Still, let's adjust Venice to 10/10. We want our vassal cooperative. But we also have Iron Fist which prevents rebellion, but positive relations can't hurt.
